**Important:** An in-code lock is a precise source snapshot. It is deliberately broad: even a harmless UI code modification will fail the check until the user has authorized it and the baseline is intentionally updated. Non-UI work is not blocked.

### 2026-10-09 — Anomalies compile and section-grouping fix (current main)

After the initial four-panel Anomalies commit, the user reported .NET compiler **CS8510** in `TabViewModel.SliderGuide`: two identical `"environment"` switch arms. The obsolete second arm was removed; the first, four-panel-aware help text remains. **Critical UI correction:** `environment` is now explicitly included in `TabViewModel.ParametersView.GroupDescriptions`, so `Global Anomaly Damage`, `Individual Anomaly Damage`, `Flycatcher Slowdown`, and `Special Anomaly Effects` render as **four distinct top-level grouped sections** rather than ungrouped cards. Existing `MainViewModel.ParameterSortOrder` keeps that exact order. Equipment help text also now accurately mentions the already-implemented interactive three-ring center. No game parameter values, IDs, source mappings, or presets are changed by this fix. A local .NET/Wine build is still needed for runtime verification.

## 2026-10-09 — Master tab: final source-accurate UI pass

**Explicit final-tab redesign approval:** Master remains five full-width source-backed groups in this precise existing order: **Starvation**, **Severe Fatigue**, **Radiation**, **Low Health**, **Medical Effect Modifier**. The existing group catalog names are unchanged; the player-facing panel titles are respectively **STARVATION PENALTIES**, **SEVERE FATIGUE**, **RADIATION PENALTY**, **LOW HEALTH PENALTY**, and **CONSUMABLE EFFECT PENALTIES**. Each of the five original parent toggles and all fifteen native child penalty/healing controls are preserved (**20 total**); the five `AdditionalMechanicsEffects` identities and fifteen dependency edges remain untouched. Master and non-Master base difficulty toggles are still source/difficulty-specific; this is not a new game difficulty.

**Unified Master visual contract:** Each section has one centered gold 12.5-DIP header and one centered 39-DIP `DrawTogglePill` with the universal 20-DIP checkbox. No repeated ON/ACTIVE text or decorative lower instructions. All numeric sliders use the new shared `DrawMasterNativeCard`: **80-DIP height**, 94-DIP pitch, bounded to **370 DIP** per card at editor widths **>=672 DIP**, or **440 DIP** single column below. Every card has one short neutral label left and exactly one native current number/right aligned on the header, original real source min/max under its own rail, the muted selected-baseline tick and a single native track with central `AddHorizontalTarget` drag/click and right-click-to-reset. Actual changed numeric glyphs **including their signs, decimal units and %/x suffixes** are accent yellow and +1.2 DIP only when differing from selected baseline; inactive/disabled child values stay muted but preserved. Entire cards, titles and rails do not glow: the existing shared `DrawHoveredHandle` overlay highlights only the hovered control point. Keyboard and R-family reset continue using the shared aggregate behavior. A click selects the actual member's right rationale through `RaiseParameterSelected` (now includes all five Master families), not only the family anchor.

1. **STARVATION PENALTIES** (`StarvationTimeline`): 1 bundle toggle plus five true sources. Two timing editors **PENALTIES START** (0–6s; native 0.5s) and **DAMAGE EVERY** (1–20s; native 1s) follow the compact two-column/one-column grid. A centered **80-DIP read-only damage tick timeline** marks the authored start delay then three repeating damage instants on the configured interval, without suggesting simulated health, effect falloff or HP/s output. Below it, three compact native editors: **EXTRA STAMINA COST** (0–400%, 0.01 source), **HP LOST PER TICK** (0–8 HP, native 0.5), **HEALTH RECOVERY BLOCKED** (0–100%, 0.01 source). This section measures **496 DIP wide / 684 DIP narrow** using the actual wrapped row count; no overlap or concealed bottom cards. All children retain their staged values when OFF.
2. **SEVERE FATIGUE** (`SevereFatiguePenaltyBoard`): toggle plus five native scalers in centered **2×3** (last card centered) or narrow single column. **MAX STAMINA LOST** (0–40%), **CARRY CAPACITY LOST** (0–40%), **EXTRA RECOIL** (+0–110%) are genuine positive player-facing effect magnitudes converted to engine-negative where required. **AIM SWAY** (0–0.8x) and **SHOT SPREAD** (0–1x) stay raw source multipliers; do not infer a weapon-accuracy result. Board height **401/589 DIP**.
3. **RADIATION PENALTY** (`MasterRadiationPenalty`): toggle plus one centered max-440-DIP native **EXTRA ACTION STAMINA COST** (+0–100%) editor. Board height **204 DIP**. It does not change radiation buildup, radiation damage or the activation threshold.
4. **LOW HEALTH PENALTY** (`MasterLowHealth`): toggle plus one centered max-440-DIP native **HEALTH RECOVERY LOSS** (−0–100%) editor. Board height **204 DIP**. The engine's authored 30-HP low-health trigger remains unchanged.
5. **CONSUMABLE EFFECT PENALTIES** (`MasterMedicalHealing`; updated 2026-10-13): toggle plus exactly three direct 0–100% native `ValueMultiplier` lanes in a **single aligned, 680-DIP-capped bank**, not the former 2×2 / one-column card arrangement. BANDAGE / MEDKIT / ARMY MEDKIT each retain their own source track, 0.01 step and muted 25%/71%/82% authored difficulty reference, with a separate quiet 100% full-healing guide. Shared bottom 0/100/200% axis, fixed **296 DIP** board height. Toggle continues to govern other authored consumable modifiers, including nonmedical effects.

**No gameplay changes in this pass.** Keep all twenty parameter IDs, catalog slider ranges/steps, references, writer operations, dependency-enabled/disabled state, preset serialization and original story/state thresholds. No derived final HP restoration is simulated. This visual-only pass concludes the tab-by-tab UI redesign; deep CFG gameplay audit is next and separate. Code-level static review done; local .NET 10/Wine validation required.

## 2026-10-09 — A-Life two-panel gamer-centric redesign (current approved implementation)

**This is the active A-Life UI design.** Two full-width top-level cards contain the same **18 source-backed controls** as before: four human player-rank squad caps and fourteen concrete mutant species encounter caps. The existing catalog groups **Human Group Size** and **Mutant Group Size** remain separate. The earlier broader 'radar' layouts, cramped tiny captions and per-row grey `vN NPCs` duplicate text are superseded by these exact compact presentations. Their class names `HumanGroupSizeRadar` and `MutantGroupSizeRadar` remain for XAML compatibility.

- **HUMAN SQUAD SIZE** (`HumanGroupSizeRadar`, **350-DIP board**): four vertically stacked independent source cards NEWBIE, EXPERIENCED, VETERAN, MASTER. A centered bounded bank (card max width **760 DIP**), card top = 50 + 72×index DIP, card height 66 DIP. One card shows rank left (10.5 DIP), a short neutral MAX SQUAD label near the right, the **actual resulting integer cap** prominently (16 DIP, +1.2 DIP when altered), and its independent native source multiplier (11 DIP, +1.2 DIP when altered), all on the upper line. A single horizontal track underneath edits the actual `alife.humanGroupSize.{rank}` source in **0.25–3x**, native **0.05x** steps; small grey native min/max are below and one grey tick marks that parameter's selected reference. Source authored vanilla caps are **3/4/5/6**, and displayed actual caps use the existing identical half-up integer policy. Only a cap **different from the selected reference cap** is highlighted yellow; a non-reference multiplier and its `x` turn yellow independently. Do not highlight numbers merely on hover or selection. Universal `ControlHoverGlow` applies to the slider **handle alone**; rail card/labels stay neutral. Click/drag and native arrow keys edit that one rank; right-click rail resets only that rank; `R` resets all four. Clicking/right-clicking a rank's rail selects its actual source for the right-side rationale via `ParameterSelected`.
- **MUTANT GROUP SIZE** (`MutantGroupSizeRadar`, **546-DIP board**): 14 real species **alphabetically ordered** in two columns of seven each. Centered content bank capped at **784 DIP**, gap 12 DIP; row top = 64 + 68×row DIP, height 61 DIP. On each species card: uppercase species name left, native source multiplier `x` right on the first line; on the second, neutral **N / E / V / M** rank legend and **four actual resulting maximum NPC counts**, then one compact horizontal native source track below. Authoring-source vanilla rank caps are preserved, including species-specific progressions and **zero caps** at disabled player ranks. Counts are independently highlighted/enlarged **only if their computed integer result differs from that rank's selected reference count**, so a retained 0 NEVER falsely turns gold. Multiplier/current track reference styling follows the same global current-number rule as Human Squad Size. Source range **0.25–3x**, native tick **0.05x**. Interaction: drag or click rail edits one species; hover glow only on its handle; right-click resets that species; `R` resets all 14; actual selected species is sent to the right rationale via `ParameterSelected`. No composite or inferred encounter probabilities.
- **A-Life boundary:** `GameData/ALifePrototypes/ALifeDirectorScenarioPrototypes.cfg` `ALifeDirectorPreset.ALifeScenarioNPCArchetypesLimitsPerPlayerRank[*].Restrictions[*].MaxCount` is the only edited domain. These cards do **not** change spawn cadence, global population, lair capacity/refill/wipe timers, story scenarios, or faction density. The hidden `alife.mutantSpawn.generic` stays hidden because generic/species effective precedence is unproven. No lair controls are promoted from `ALIFE-LAIR-AUDIT.md`. All parameter IDs, writer transformations, selected-difficulty references and presets are unchanged.

`MainWindow.xaml` explicitly matches the two measured board heights (350/546 DIP) and wires both `ParameterSelected` handlers to the existing right-side rationale router. Avoid duplicating row/group titles or reintroducing low-contrast sub-9-DIP label text. Local .NET and Wine acceptance pending.

## 2026-10-09 — Anomalies gamer-centric four-panel redesign (explicitly approved)

**Authoritative for the Anomalies tab.** Supersedes UI-02's old six-vertical-fader Anomaly Effects mixer, any obsolete 'global immediately before individual at bottom' ordering, and the historical one-section Anomalies catalog groups. The tab still has exactly **17 native gameplay parameters**, stored under its unchanged `environment` ID, split into four independently visible **full-width** group cards, in this order:

1. **GLOBAL ANOMALY DAMAGE** — `AnomalyGlobalDamage`, one centered 270° **OVERALL DAMAGE MULTIPLIER** rotary knob (194×181 DIP inner card; **242-DIP** board), directly editing `environment.anomalyDamage` (0–2.5x, native 0.01x steps). The selected difficulty reference remains Novice 0.30x / Stalker 1.00x / Veteran 1.45x / Master 1.75x. The muted reference tick does not write to individual anomaly sources. The 2026-10-11 knob contract above supersedes the former horizontal slider.
2. **INDIVIDUAL ANOMALY DAMAGE** — existing `AnomalyDamageRadar` with all **eleven** original controls, arranged in compact 2 columns × 6 rows. Each row displays its raw native value (Razor's separate damage scaler displays `x`), and a normalized **0–200% of that row's own vanilla source** rail backed by the original source range and native tick. The two-column content bank is capped at 756 DIP; a 52-DIP pitch and readable 10.2-DIP label / 10.3–11.5-DIP value replace overly small text, without new math. A row click/drag/right-click selects that exact member's right-hand rationale instead of leaving the Electro anchor selected. Do NOT label the rail a final effective HP output or include player protection as if its result were confirmed. 420-DIP board.
3. **FLYCATCHER SLOWDOWN** — `FlycatcherSlowdown` is a centered square two-axis Combat-style plot, 185–230 DIP side, board `147 + side` DIP. Its three **matching gold ring handles** come from `DrawHumanAxisHandle`: bottom edits retained **movement speed** 0–100%; left edits retained **turn speed** 0–100%; **central joint edits both** independently from X/Y coordinates. Raw source step remains 0.01 (1 percentage point); vanilla retained speed = 50% movement and 30% turn. Native negative CFG transformation `source−1` remains unchanged, including joint yaw/pitch turn mapping. Names and changed-only yellow values attach to the axes like Human Combat (top X, vertically rotated Y), with min/middle/max percent scale labels, neutral dotted grid, muted grey reference ghost and yellow current guides. Hit areas and hover-only circle glow must match Combat; center right-click resets both, outer right-click resets only own, R resets both. No fictitious slowing/duration simulation.
4. **SPECIAL ANOMALY EFFECTS** — `AnomalySpecialEffects`, three compact horizontal `DrawEconomyCard` sources: `environment.razorBleeding` (0–2x, 0.01x), `environment.diamondBleeding` (**0–20 actual bleed points**, 0.5 step), `environment.psyAnomalyIntensity` (0–2x, 0.01x). Use 370-DIP-capped side-by-side/centered short-final-row banks at editor width >=672; 440-DIP single-column below. Board height 248 or 342 DIP. Razor's low/high source effect tiers and Psy's source-negative drain contract are unchanged. No artificial total damage output.

**Shared rules:** neutral static labels, muted selected-difficulty reference ticks, numbers **with %/x/pts suffix** turn gold and grow ~1.2 DIP only when source-modified. Glow only on hovered handles; no section repeats 'MIXER', unnecessary Base badges, explanatory footer, or duplicate controls. Grouping changes only the catalog `group` presentation metadata, never IDs, ranges, saved preset keys, writer routes or parameter targets. Every aggregate has its own anchor and group R-reset scope. Damage radar is a distinct source-bound visual and retains its native 11-member reset scope. Weather/emissions and other non-damage anomaly behaviors remain deliberately excluded. Source mapping proof and runtime CFG semantics remain in `ANOMALIES-MAPPING.md`; local WPF/Wine QA remains pending.

## 2026-10-09 — Equipment tab: latest explicitly approved lock

**Supersedes the older Equipment crosshair dimensions, inner explanatory footer and Item Weight vertical-mixer contract.** The normal Equipment tab contains **exactly four player-facing source parameters in two full-width top-level rows**:

1. **EQUIPMENT WEAR** (`AggregateVisualControl / EquipmentWearBalance`) now matches the **actual Human Combat 2D graph** presentation and interaction, not the earlier non-interactive preview. The **square plot is centered** (size computed from actual editor width as `min(240, max(200, width − 360))` DIP), top at 86 DIP; its measured section height is `147 + plotSide` DIP (347–387 DIP). **Both axes show their concise name plus one live source value inline**: horizontal ARMOR TOUGHNESS above the graph, vertically rotated WEAPON WEAR / SHOT to its left. No detached value cards, repeated labels or bottom instruction legend. Both use Combat's native tick grid (five lines; min/mid/max visible in neutral), gold dotted current X/Y guides and muted grey dashed selected-difficulty guides with a grey ghost intersection. Current numeric value including `x` is gold and +1.2 DIP only if changed from selected baseline; static labels and axis ticks remain neutral. This visual copies `DrawHumanAxisHandle` from Combat **for ALL THREE identical circular handle glyphs** with the same hover-only `ControlHoverGlow` and 34×22 / 27×28 / 36×36 hit rectangles. The **bottom gold ring** edits only X = `equipment.armorDurability` (0.10–2x, native 0.01 step); the **left gold ring** edits only Y = `equipment.weaponWearPerShot` (0–2x, native 0.01 step); the **center gold ring is now INTERACTIVE** and edits both independently via its native X/Y coordinate mapping, exactly like Human Combat. Empty plot space remains noninteractive. Center right-click resets both source values (outside ring right-click resets only its own); `R` resets the entire Equipment Wear family. Drag/click with snapping, separate axis keyboard editing and hover/focus states follow Combat's shared `HitKind.HumanJoint` / source-specific axis behavior. No fake derived armor wear-speed formula, no changed weapon jamming, no new parameters/preset or CFG mappings. The exact armor wear conversion remains runtime-test-required.

2. **ITEM WEIGHT** (`AggregateVisualControl / ItemWeightBoard`) uses **two ordinary compact horizontal cards** via `DrawEconomyCard`, never vertical faders. `equipment.ammoWeight` and `equipment.consumableWeight` retain 0–2x, native 0.01x steps, reference marker, direct hit rail/handle, one-source right click and group R reset, writer routes and presets. Names AMMO WEIGHT and FOOD & MEDICINE sit left; live `x` value sits right in the same bounded header line. The two cards are centered with max 370 DIP each in 2 columns at >=672 DIP editor width, or max 440 DIP in a single column below; 80-DIP cards and 94-DIP row pitch give 154-/248-DIP measured boards. Gold appears only on modified number+unit and the interactive handle; glow is handle-only.

**Equipment Bonus Caps:** `RegenStamina.MaxValue`, `DegenBleeding.MaxValue`, `AdditionalInventoryWeight.MaxValue` and `PenaltyLessWeight.MaxValue` are **audit-only, not player-facing controls**. Their upper-bound CFG meaning is known, but their complete in-game impact and interactions are not established. Do not add them or simulate benefits. The current live `parameters.json` already contains **only the four player-facing Equipment parameters**; the former eight-control `v0.2.49` Equipment audit documents a historical surface, **not** current UI requirements. The excluded caps' prior research is retained in documentation; no writer/catalog parameters are invented or silently removed for this redesign.

No change to item weight material sources, 34 ammo items, 22+1 food/medicine items, 41+11 player weapons, source limits, selected vanilla difficulty reference, presets or BPatch writer semantics. All existing non-Equipment UI locks remain binding. Wine/WPF acceptance is pending a local build/test.

### 2026-10-08 — UI-09 — reject stale Wine process during publish

**User report:** The running application still shows Attack Frequency 4x and Fire Accuracy 2x, contrary to the approved 2x/1x limits. The embedded catalog, `ParameterViewModel.Maximum`, and Human Combat source already enforce 2x/1x on `main`; do not alter unrelated controls or falsely claim these values were missing in the source.

**Implementation:** `publish.sh` now refuses to publish while an existing `TrueCustomDifficulty.exe` Wine process is active, displays the detected process and asks for it to be closed. This prevents testing the pre-existing 4x/2x window while believing a freshly published EXE is running. The existing `run-latest.sh` independently verifies source commit and EXE checksum. UI implementation and UI lock snapshots are unchanged. Actual Wine runtime acceptance still requires the user to close prior windows, pull `main`, publish and launch the new EXE.

### 2026-10-08 — UI-09 — fix stale EXE detection in publish preflight

**Screenshot remains unaccepted:** Attack Frequency shows 4x, Fire Accuracy shows 2x, and the legacy square/preview glyphs remain visible. The approved runtime bounds are 2x/1x. The previous `publish.sh` process check accidentally used a doubled backslash in its `pgrep -f` regular expression, which could miss an already-running Wine executable. Corrected both matches to `[T]rueCustomDifficulty\.exe` (single regex escape before the period). Publishing now fails before replacing output while the named process is present. This is a publish/verification defect correction, not a change to locked UI geometry or to CFG semantics. Visual/runtime acceptance is still pending a genuinely fresh launch; inspect `publish/win-x64/BUILD-INFO.txt` and run `./run-latest.sh` after closing old windows.

### 2026-10-08 — Unified Hunger Effect Curve (supersedes dual graphs)

### 2026-10-09 — Source-aware numeric-only highlighting in compound captions

**General design rule:** where gamer-facing labels combine dynamic CFG numbers with explanatory words/units, never color the entire caption yellow simply because one source differs from its reference. Use a reusable `DrawCenteredNumericChanges` text-rendering helper that takes the ordered numeric tokens' individual modified-from-reference flags. Only the digits of each modified numeric token are accented; other digits, `LOW/MEDIUM/SEVERE`, `AT`, `PTS`, `STAMINA RECOVERY`, delimiters, minus and percent symbols remain normal. Do not automatically highlight numbers in static scales, axis limits or labels without a parameter association. Applied now to Hunger State Ladder's three stage-preview readouts and its three bottom Hunger Survival Profile stage captions, with independent change flags for each stage's threshold and recovery loss. Never infer modification from a combined sentence-wide Boolean. Existing Hunger geometry, lock, hit areas and native CFG values are unchanged. This helper is the standard for other affected compound text when updating those renderers, rather than indiscriminately recoloring literals. Pending local build/Wine verification.

### 2026-10-09 — Unified source-aware numeric highlighting across value captions

User requested that **all dynamic CFG-value captions** follow the Hunger rule: label words, units, signs, `%`, `x`, `HP`, `PTS`, separators, `BASE` and unmodified/reference numbers remain in their original neutral text colors. Only the digits of a value that differs from the parameter's selected reference receive accent yellow. Source changes must be checked **per parameter**, never by painting an entire mixed sentence. Static axes, scale limits, section headers and explanatory text do not represent changed source values and are not highlighted. Implemented a reusable formatted-text source-number renderer in `AggregateVisualControl` and applied it to the common compact/horizontal track components, Hunger stage preview/footers, Master medical and penalty readouts, Bleeding Treatment, Player Weapons, Bleeding curve stage captions, and Mutant Health live value/HP readouts. Existing custom Consumables statistic lines already color independent numeric values; no geometry, native CFG values, or locked board layouts were changed. Other specialized renderer captions should follow the same established pattern when touched; they must not be blanket-colored or falsely presented as changed without a source comparison. ### 2026-10-09 — Economy tab full compact redesign (explicit user approval)

**Current UI contract; supersedes the prior Economy-specific vertical-fader, long-form price table and standalone coupon-row layouts.** All **22 distinct player-facing Economy sources** remain available from six full-width top-level cards, with no duplicate group header, status legend, instructional footer or unverified price simulation:

### 2026-10-09 — Economy dense-grid polish (latest Economy implementation)

All common cards are 80 DIP high with 94-DIP row pitch and 12-DIP gap; measured board heights follow 154 DIP for a single row, 248 DIP for two rows and 436 DIP for four narrow rows. Each category title reserves the actual width of its right-aligned current multiplier and gets one-line ellipsis if necessary. No simulated item buy/sell payouts, trader stock quantities or repair totals were introduced. Local .NET/Wine compilation and visual acceptance pending.

### 2026-10-09 — Global Stamina Usage slider anatomy and neutral references (latest user-approved rule)

**This entry supersedes conflicting older slider-value positions and any requirement to visually emphasize Vanilla/Base values across ALL tabs.** For every ordinary horizontally editable source slider, use one coherent, non-overlapping top line: **source name on the left, editable CURRENT value right-aligned** (shared card left/right padding and reserved space so the label cannot run beneath the current value); place the continuous interactive rail directly below that line, and display any native min/max labels underneath the rail in the same neutral small typography. Do not alternate between left-justified current values on the next line, right-justified current values in the same family, or side-by-side current/Base figures. When a separate preview/semantic graph needs read-only numeric results, keep those unambiguously labeled as derived results rather than a second slider value.

Current source numbers remain text-colored at the selected reference and turn gold **only after a real source deviation**; modified current numbers grow by +1.2 DIP while all units, bounds, captions and explanatory text stay neutral. The interactive rail/handle can retain its gold affordance, but on hover only the **handle** glows. Never color a full card or the displayed value merely because the mouse hovers. **Vanilla/Base references are not highlighted**: retain a thin, unobtrusive grey tick for source comparison and native snapping/reset where already supported; where standard `VanillaMarkerBar` displays clickable reference labels, keep the labels/dots muted even during hover. Do not place gold/white duplicate Base numbers or instructive legends in card headers or footers.

The reusable `AggregateVisualControl.DrawHorizontalTrackCard` and `DrawCompactTrack` now follow this exact hierarchy. This affects shared health, stamina, fatigue, survival, loot, equipment and economy layouts without changing source bindings or hit-target semantics. Two-axis profiles keep their currently rendered independent value labels; the unused `DrawDualParameterHeading` helper has been retired. Existing Trading, Reputation Repair, Coupon, Service, Stock and Item Value cards already place the live value on the right. The compact vertical mixers, native semantic curves, separate multi-axis controls and Mutant Health's previously approved **species left / multiplier centered / actual HP right** remain differentiated where the meaningful control structure requires it; their **reference readouts still stay muted**, and vertical mixer editable numbers are no longer yellow at Base. Dedicated Action Costs, Medical Healing, Special Ability, Human/Mutant group, Anomaly Damage, Mutant Drops and World Loot horizontal rows use the same changed-number emphasis rule.

This is a visual presentation change, not a global rescale: preserve sliders' native min/max, source/CFG stepping and mapping, actual Base reference values, value-based cascades, right-click/R reset, selected-parameter rationale, preset round trips and writer output. Do not substitute a universal 1x or mask Base values in internal calculations. Local publish and Wine inspection are required to verify text widths and min-width layouts; do not claim they passed without running them.

### 2026-10-09 — One universal hover glow across every interactive control

**User-approved global visual change; supersedes all earlier per-family hover halo sizes, opacities, double-rings, whole-card hover backgrounds and selection-only glow.** All hand-drawn editor handles use the single helper `Controls/ControlHoverGlow.cs` with **halo radius 15 DIP / opacity 0.17** and **outline ring radius 11 DIP / opacity 0.67 / 1.6-DIP stroke**, drawn using the existing gold AccentBrush. Hover state alone enables it. Selected/focused/modified-but-not-hovered controls do not manufacture this glow.

- Shared aggregate renderers determine the current editable handle position from the actual source mapping and show the halo **only on the hovered active hit target**. Combined graph rings reuse the same helper at their actual drawn point. Toggles receive the same indicator-sized halo, not a glowing entire panel. Hovering a Base/Vanilla reference tick, selection-only area, static preview or noninteractive legend does not glow or edit values.
- Ordinary WPF controls use `UniversalHoverGlowEffect` in `App.xaml`, matching gold, radius 15 and opacity 0.67 with zero shadow depth. The same resource drives Button/PrimaryButton/window buttons, preset ComboBoxes and their options, TabItem, TextBox, CheckBox switch **thumb**, native Slider **thumb** and wheel-editable value box. On slider mouseover the track, label and current numeric text stay neutral; only the thumb gets hover effect. The ordinary parameter-card background must not change because a mouse is merely over the card.
- Disabled controls remain non-editable and do not receive active handle halos. Preserve focus styling, keyboard/touch/mouse behavior, native snapping, preset reset, CFG and value-reference calculations. Handle-only hover is strictly visual; no new gameplay parameter or hit surface.

The canonical drawing parameters are deliberately fixed in one class and the WPF equivalent is a single named effect resource. Do not introduce another custom blur, radius, selector-opacity or full-card hover wash in later UI work without an explicit user-approved change. Local publish + Wine acceptance is still required.

### 2026-10-09 — Universal minimal checkbox design (explicit approval; latest rule)

**Supersedes the earlier WPF pill switch, per-panel checkbox shapes, ON/OFF status-lane pills and the 2026-10-09 global hover-halo requirement specifically for boolean checkboxes.** Across all tabs, every ordinary boolean input renders as the same compact **20 × 20 DIP square**, 3-DIP corner radius, 1-DIP neutral border and dark `PanelAltBrush` face. The checked state is communicated **only by the gold checkmark** (two rounded 2.2-DIP strokes), not by a yellow card, yellow text, yellow track, extra ON/OFF caption or a glowing outline. The unchecked state has an empty square. Static control names remain neutral and adjacent to the box; long names fit in the remaining width without overlapping it.

No ON/OFF label highlighting. Keyboard focus and checked state remain distinct from hover; disabled checkboxes are not editable. The broader universal `ControlHoverGlow` continues to govern sliders and graph handles; **checkboxes are deliberately exempt**.

**Single implementation contract:** WPF's global `CheckBox` template in `App.xaml` and the drawn `BooleanCheckboxChrome.Draw` in `Controls/BooleanCheckboxChrome.cs` share exactly the same size, corner radius, border, stroke, color and hover behavior. `AggregateVisualControl.DrawTogglePill` uses this chrome for all toggle-led panels (World Loot Layout, bundle toggles, healing/radiation/fatigue, starvation, equipment mechanics); `MedicalHealingRadar` uses it for Food Healing. No old slider-style toggle halo overlay may activate for `HitKind.Toggle`. Regular WPF parameter checkbox rows keep one label at left and the same square at right, without repeating ON/OFF.

**Nonvisual behavior unchanged:** each boolean retains its real `ParameterViewModel.CurrentBoolean`, enabled state, existing clickable row/target, click/Space/keyboard selection, right-click reference reset, visual-family R reset, preset persistence, source catalog and CFG writing. The Food Healing card remains clickable in its existing hit area, but only hovering the 20-DIP checkbox brightens its border. No changes to sliders, graphs, native step limits or gameplay values. Inspect the WPF template and draw-based panels under Wine; source/UI geometry checks are not a substitute for a runtime build.

### 2026-10-09 — CFG-first, gamer-centric visual controls (global design authorization)

**User-approved direction for the ongoing tab-by-tab redesign.** Stamina Usage defines the legibility of source values, but **does not require every setting to become a horizontal slider**. Choose the control or chart that most faithfully represents what the actual editable CFG fields do, and that lets a player understand the gameplay trade-off without reading a long caption. The source/cfg contract decides the visualization—not decoration, novelty, or a fixed quota of control types.

**Selection contract for new and revised controls:**

- **One independent scalar** (linear multiplier, chance, quantity, seconds, raw damage): one compact native-range slider with current value, units, neutral endpoints and subdued reference marker; avoid oversized gauges for simple values.
- **Two independent variables with a meaningful combined result**: an editable square X/Y plot, with separate axis handles and a joint handle only if both can really be edited independently. The coordinates and axes must map exactly to the two native sources.
- **Thresholds, stages, activation/recovery and value-dependent state transitions**: an editable stepped profile, segmented threshold chart or native-value timeline. Nodes/steps correspond to actual CFG thresholds/effects; do not interpolate authored discrete states into an invented smooth curve.
- **Several directly comparable source channels**: grouped lanes, compact horizontal banks, a stacked/range bar or a radar only if all plotted values share a meaningful comparable scale and proper min/max/source semantics. Avoid radar for unrelated values or deceptive normalization.
- **Paired minimum/maximum values and hard ordering constraints**: one range editor with two identifiable handles, based on actual CFG values, not percentages of unrelated baselines. Preserve active-handle priority and only the necessary cascades.
- **Real duration or event sequences**: an editable timeline only where a CFG source contains timing/interval/duration. Mark deterministic versus probabilistic results distinctly; never imply a guaranteed event cadence without source evidence.
- **Binary states**: the existing universal 20-DIP minimal checkbox with restrained neutral-border hover; no unnecessary switch graph or ON/OFF repetition.
- **Discrete candidates or enumerations**: compact selection/segmented-choice controls bound to the exact permitted CFG options, not pseudo-continuous faders.
- **Read-only calculated values**: a secondary clearly distinguished preview, only if deterministically derived from audited source values; it must never silently become another editable parameter or suggest a verified runtime formula when none exists.

Keep all existing design locks unless a specific redesign is approved. All controls—simple or advanced—must reuse the common gold-handle hover, neutral static labels and reference markers, source-modified numeric emphasis (+1.2 DIP), accessible hit areas, clear gameplay-centric units, native steps, reset/keyboard behavior, and full documentation. Prefer one obvious visual representation over repeated explanatory text; avoid clipped labels and dead space. No change to underlying CFG semantics, source paths, difficulty overlays, presets, or writer is authorized by this policy alone. Sketch a proposed semantic redesign **before implementing the next tab**, then make the accepted code and both catalogs match in one `main` commit. New charts require checking actual source parameter semantics, not inventing simulated DPS, loot guarantees, enemy attack rates, final prices or other runtime quantities absent from audited CFG.

### 2026-10-09 — Survival tab gamer-centric CFG-first redesign (approved; current)

**This is the authoritative current Survival UI contract.** It supersedes the older "read-only duration preview + five vertical duration faders", "smooth bleeding-damage curve", "long one-line fatigue captions", "three intoxication tiles", and overly small stage/axis typography. No change to source IDs, CFG write paths, native min/max/steps, reset/presets, select-difficulty reference numbers, gameplay calculations, or established constraint cascades.

1. **Hunger State Ladder** (`HungerLadder`): preserve the accepted synchronized 0–100 HungerPoints preview and threshold-linked *discrete* stamina-recovery loss profile. The three thresholds stay native editing sources and Medium/Severe loss remains true positive stored magnitude / negative UI effect. Keep live stage labels in fixed cells and avoid smoothing authored step changes. Improve the stage preview heading to **11.0 DIP**. No fabricated elapsed-time-to-hunger simulation.
2. **Fatigue Response** (`FatigueCurve`): keep the two independent, real-Y-scale gold AIM TIME and pale RELOAD TIME stepped profiles with shared real 0–100 SleepinessPoints thresholds; LOW has no reload source. Preserve the separate white Y-only reload handles. Widen the footer vertically from one clipped sentence to one fixed **four-line-capable** stage cell in each third: LOW/MEDIUM/SEVERE label; `AT N PTS`; `AIM +N%`; where authored, `RELOAD +N%`. No forced LOW reload. The actual frame and `CompactFatigueHeight` must both use **365 DIP** instead of 320; lines at frame Y+262 / +282 / +304 / +326 and cells Y+260 height93, safely below X-axis ticks. Use 11-DIP stage preview headings and 10.2-DIP real axis captions. Golden modified numbers only when the corresponding source differs from the difficulty reference.
3. **Consumable Effects** (`ConsumableMixer`): retain four independent 0–2x top strength sliders with original native 0.05 steps, and three concise *read-only* effect readouts for Water/Energy/Limited Energy instant stamina, Water/Energy sustained buffs and Beer/Vodka radiation removal. The effect readouts now place **one effect per line** rather than clipping pipe-separated sentences; up to three 9.8-DIP effect lines under a 10.4-DIP title, highlighting/enlarging only changed numeric tokens by +1.2 DIP. No extra duplicate readout headings.
   
   **Replace all five vertical duration faders and the read-only longest-effect preview with one directly editable common-seconds timeline.** Five horizontally independent native source lanes are labeled WATER, ENERGY, VODKA, BEER and MAGIC VODKA. Its common **0–270 real-second** ruler shows 0/90/180/270 seconds. Proven longest authored duration prototypes are **30 / 45 / 20 / 20 / 90 seconds**, respectively, scaled by the same original native **0–3x multiplier** and **0.05x step**. Each lane renders an interactive gold handle at `SecondsX(authoredSeconds × currentMultiplier)`, a thin neutral selected-reference tick, a subdued uneditable tail *beyond that lane's own native maximum*, and a right-aligned exact current longest duration in seconds. WATER's shorter 5-second effect and ENERGY's shorter 5/15-second effects appear only as passive minor ticks at their scaled times, **not as independent controls**. Do not imply all five families can last 270 seconds. Native hit mapping uses the lane's physically shorter `0..authoredSeconds*3` track, rather than stretching the source multiplier across the entire 270s axis; any grey tail cannot receive hover/edit. `AddHorizontalTarget` retains source-native snapping, track selection, focused arrows and right-click/R reset. There must be **exactly nine editable sources** (four strengths + five durations), not duplicated read-only and interactive duration handles. Remove unused `DrawConsumableFader` and `ConsumableFaderColumns`, and measure actual new panel bottom as `previewTop + ConsumableTimelineHeight + 14` rather than retaining the stale 179-DIP fader bank.
4. **Alcohol Tolerance / Drunkenness** (`DrunkennessControl`): keep the two native Beer/Vodka strength sliders and their deterministic already-audited number-of-drinks stage previews. The progression captions use legible ~10-DIP type. The existing hidden `survival.drunknessThresholdScale` stays hidden and OverDrunkness is not folded into the graph. Static stage boundaries and labels are muted, the source value and derived WASTED-after-N count are gold and +1.2 DIP **only if the corresponding Beer/Vodka source is modified**, and the old yellow VANILLA baseline caption disappears; its muted source-reference tick remains. **Shared invariants:** current values remain neutral at the selected vanilla difficulty reference; yellow and +1.2-DIP size apply only to source-modified numeric tokens; thin reference marks never receive highlight; labels do not overlap or turn gold solely on hover; all handle halos use `ControlHoverGlow` and boolean editors remain universal minimal checkboxes. ### 2026-10-09 — Approved Stamina four-panel wireframe (implemented on main)

**Authoritative Stamina tab contract, superseding older single 393-DIP `STAMINA USAGE` + generic Water/Exhaustion notes.** Exactly four top-level cards remain in this order: **STAMINA & MOVEMENT → STAMINA USAGE → EXHAUSTION & RECOVERY → WATER STAMINA DRAIN**. This implementation adopts the approved 16:9 pre-commit sketch, using native CFG sources and compact editing, not extra preview knobs.

1. **STAMINA & MOVEMENT** (`StaminaCore`, 259 DIP) remains the approved 2×2 compact board: Capacity (`stamina.maxSP`, STAMINA), Base Recovery (`stamina.regeneration`, STAMINA/s), Vault Height (`stamina.vaultMaxHeight`, body reference landmarks), Carry Weight (`stamina.maxCarryWeight`, kg). The vault landmarks remain clickable and centered on their exact native source marks. No repeated Base/Vanilla text.
2. **STAMINA USAGE** (`ActionCostsRadar`, `Mode=Usage`, 207 DIP) displays **only six** direct one-time STAMINA costs, in two compact columns: **MOVEMENT** = Sprint, Jump, Vault; **MELEE** = Normal Melee, Strong Melee, Buttstock. These six rails preserve the previous relative-to-selected-reference 0–2x interaction coordinate but show the current native **STAMINA** amount, never an invented STAMINA/s, percent or cumulative consumption. Native CFG min/max/step and the normal ≤ strong melee value-order rule remain authoritative. Current numeric tokens are neutral at reference and accent only on deviation.
3. **EXHAUSTION & RECOVERY** (`ActionCostsRadar`, `Mode=Exhaustion`, 330 DIP wide/450 DIP narrow) uses one STAMINA THRESHOLDS card (SPRINT STOPS BELOW and BOOST STARTS BELOW on the shared default 0–100 STAMINA ruler, expanding if Max Stamina exceeds 100) plus three compact recovery groups; native edit limits remain independent (0–32.2 and 1–60). Above-maximum lane tails are non-clickable; right-side Aiming, After Exhaustion and Low-Stamina Boost controls are independently editable.
4. **WATER STAMINA DRAIN** (`WaterStaminaPressure`, 149 DIP) has two Stamina-style horizontal native cards: Medium Water (`stamina.waterMediumDrain`, 0–2x) and Deep Water (`stamina.waterStrongDrain`, 0–3x). Moving either edits its own source, and the existing value-based constraint guarantees Medium ≤ Deep; when one is moved, the linked neighbor changes only as much as needed. No radiation chart, fabricated common percent axis, decorative footer or duplicated number/baseline.

**Behavioral invariant:** 18 independently editable existing Stamina parameters, 18 original CFG writer routes and identical saved preset schema; the five internal movement-state stamina coefficient parameters stay documented/hidden. Right-click resets only the targeted source; R resets only the currently focused six-source Stamina Usage or Exhaustion group, and the aggregate cards retain their own normal reset scope. Grey reference marks remain unobtrusive; universal gold hover glow applies **only** to actual active handles. No full-card/text hover highlighting; labels, native units and fixed scale ticks remain neutral. Modified current **numeric tokens alone** (with attached +/-/%,/x) turn gold and gain 1.2 DIP. All ordinary source rows follow the source-name-left/current-value-right/rail-below anatomy where their width allows; shared threshold rows deliberately separate label and live number from the source-bound lane. No simulated exhaustion timelines or threshold coupling.

**QA required:** after `./publish.sh`, inspect the four cards and their order in Wine at normal/minimum window widths; drag every source, verify native step and reference reset, hit-test sprint tail beyond 32.2 STAMINA (must not respond), test mouse R reset isolation between Usage and Exhaustion, and confirm saving/loading/building retains all 18 values. Static source checks do not substitute for Wine validation.

### 2026-10-10 — Global right-hand rationale and CONTROLS pass (authoritative)

**Source of truth:** `MainWindow.xaml`, `ParameterViewModel.RationaleDisplay` and `ParameterViewModel.ControlHelp`. This updates the presentation of every gameplay tab without changing any CFG/preset parameter, values, calculations, writer route, sliders, hit targets, or actual reset behavior.

**Required reading order from top to bottom:** selected control name; WHAT IT DOES (**gameplay explanation only**); conditionally WHAT THIS VALUE MEANS; HIGHER / LOWER; WORKS WITH; CURRENT RESULT; one **CONTROLS** section; then the existing muted `TabSummary` context at the very bottom. All conditional sections retain their previous behavior. The interaction instructions **must not** be appended inside `RationaleDisplay`, and the title `CONTROLS` is authored **exactly once** in XAML, never emitted by a view-model string.

**Player-friendly controls grammar:** keep CONTROLS compact and plainly actionable (what to click/drag, what the control changes, how to reset); avoid CFG identifiers, explanation repetition, two full help paragraphs, generic repeated Vanilla/Base blurbs, duplicated headings and fake UI interactions. Use concise specialized guidance for **all 33** current `VisualAggregateFamily` types, including when the selected item is a child inside its group; use equivalent semantic guidance for specialized editors (mutant health/drop/group, stamina costs/recovery, medical healing, anomalies, world loot, etc.). Generic slider/range/choice/toggle fallbacks apply to ordinary controls. The guide is normal foreground text, not a bright callout, with a small single accent heading and no extra large card. Reuse existing right-column scrolling and responsive layout.

**Muted bottom text:** keep the short `TabSummary` only. Remove the old `SliderGuide` from the visible footer; it repeated instructions already covered by CONTROLS and conflicted with individual semantic controls. Do not put it back as a redundant long paragraph.

### 2026-10-10 — Starvation OFF must disable its visual-anchor slider

**Bug observed in Wine UI:** Under MASTER → STARVATION PENALTIES, the EXTRA STAMINA COST slider stayed gold and interactive even with STARVATION MECHANICS OFF. Unlike the four other child sliders, the stamina-cost parameter (`survival.starvationStaminaDrain`) is also the visible `StarvationTimeline` **visual aggregate anchor**. The earlier `ParameterViewModel.IsControlEnabled` expression bypassed every dependency check for aggregate anchors, including the anchor's own `requiresToggle: advanced.starvationMechanics`.

**Correct contract:** `IsControlEnabled` always honors the real `requiresToggle` provider; being a visual anchor grants visibility, **never edit permission**. `IsControlVisible` separately preserves the Starvation composite row while the parent is OFF. OFF leaves **all five child values visible, grey and non-interactive** (no gold accent, mouse drag, keyboard edit, or child CFG output). Switching ON reactivates all five using their preserved settings. The independent parent toggle remains clickable. This is also the correct pattern for future dependency-gated visual anchors.

**Implementation:** `ParameterViewModel.IsControlEnabled` no longer ORs `IsVisualAggregateAnchor`. Existing `DrawMasterNativeCard` already uses `IsControlEnabled` to draw the active accent and handles, and `AddHorizontalTarget` rejects disabled hit areas. Added safety guards for stale keyboard selection, mouse hit targets and captured drags that could survive a parent toggle transition. No gameplay number, difficulty baseline, source CFG target, writer algorithm, control geometry or formatting has changed.

**Regression:** `tests/SurvivalPatchContract` now includes the Starvation anchor in its Veteran-disabled and Master-OFF/re-enable checks; the entire five-child dependency set must disable/re-enable together, keep its anchor row visible, preserve modified values and produce no child writer edit while OFF. The production suite adds two scenarios (from 85 to 87 based on the prior inventory). Static audit: `python3 tools/audit_master_dependency_ui.py`; both this source gate and actual Windows .NET suite should be run before release. The source inspection alone is **not** a successful WPF build or Wine/in-game visual acceptance.

The dedicated `loot.looseUniqueWeaponShuffle` control was already implemented in the World Placement UI and in `CfgPatchWriterService.WriteLooseUniqueWeaponShuffle`, but the central `cfg-writer-baseline.json` still declared **244** supported parameters and lacked its manifest-backed production route. This made the actual C# `ValidateBaseline` fail before export, and the Python audits report the parameter as unsupported. **Do not remove, defer or fake a single-field CFG edit for this control.**

**Correct current inventory:** **245** catalog entries, **152 visible aggregate anchors/standalone controls**, **93 hidden backing IDs**, **232 effective editable source dimensions**, **13 intentionally non-UI source IDs**, **80 hidden-but-editable backings**. The source-backed direct/special writer route union covers **245/245**, including the new nine-actor shuffle.

**Canonical production route:** `cfg-writer-baseline.json.special.looseUniqueWeaponShuffle` contains the real `parameterId`, embedded `manifestResource` and `expectedActorCount=9`. `CfgWriterBaseline` deserializes this exact route and `CfgPatchWriterService` validates it, includes it in supported count and uses its embedded manifest in the actual writer. The writer remains a real per-actor `{bpatch}` derangement of nine physical world pickups (five item/presentation fields per actor, no `removenode`), separate from regular World Weapon Shuffle.

**Regression gates:** `tools/audit_ui_cfg_contract.py` and `tools/audit_project_consistency.py` now agree on the new totals and discover the new special route. The project audit additionally verifies route identity, manifest resource, exactly nine distinct actor/item pairs and linkage to the real C# writer. `tests/LootWorldPlacementContract` checks that the loaded production baseline contains the precise route and matches the live catalog count; its existing **24 writer scenarios** already cover unique-only, DLC, mixed weapon and other placements. Run the two Python audits, then the Windows `.NET` writer tests and Wine application. Source/structure verification alone does not prove local publish or UE runtime acceptance.
